# Multi-Level Work Breakdown Structure (WBS)

> **Companion design document**. This document is a companion to
> [RFC-039 Compiler Architecture Refactoring](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md). It
> expands the RFC's phase sequence into **independently committable and independently verifiable**
> third-level tasks.
>
> The phase order and acceptance gates are governed solely by the main text of RFC-039. This
> document is responsible only for decomposing the work to an executable granularity and recording
> conflicts that remain undecided.

**Reserved numbers (not reclaimed)**: P4 has no `4.7`/`4.8`, and P8 has no `8.1`. None of the three
has had a corresponding entry since the WBS was established: throughout this document's history,
there have been zero matches for the `**4.7` / `**4.8` / `**8.1` headings
(`git log -S'**4.7' -- docs/src/dev/architecture/09-execution-wbs.md` can verify this), and the
repository contains no references to them. `8.1` corresponds precisely to phase 1 of `05` (test
revival and completion): that phase has been split into P1 (`1.1`–`1.3`) and `8.8`, so P8's
numbering aligns with its phase number (phase 0 → `8.0`, phase 2 → `8.2`, and so on).

**Covered by this document**: task decomposition, dependency relationships, parallelization groups,
and binding of acceptance criteria.

**Not covered by this document**: the design rationale for individual tasks (see `02`–`08`) or the
rationale for phase ordering (see RFC-039).

**Registration convention (effective 2026-10-12)**: newly registered second-level/third-level rows
**must contain two fields**: **Owning phase** (the P to which the task truly belongs, which may
differ from the containing section) and **Blocks critical path**. `Awaiting scheduling` is no longer
the default state; an unmarked task is deemed **not schedulable**. Numbers must be **unique
throughout the document** (tasks on separate tracks use the track number, such as `029g.x`, and must
not occupy `N.x`). **Checkoff/completion registration is based on “task ID + evidence (gate metric,
run ID, issue number, file:line)” and must not record commit hashes**—`pull --rebase` rewrites local
hashes, so hard-coded hashes inevitably become invalid (2026-10-13 example:
`94ad03ce4`→`631eb3e3c`). Use `git log` to find commit hashes.

## Current State: Gaps Discovered During Decomposition

While consolidating the “Implementation Points” from eight documents into the main table, **five
hard gaps in the documentation system itself** were discovered. All have now converged:

| #   | Gap                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Resolution                                                                                                                                                            |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| G1  | **P5 has no implementation steps whatsoever.** RFC-039 defines P5 (checker splitting) and its acceptance criterion (C1 zero-diff), but none of the seven documents describes how to perform the split; `01-routing.md` contains a single reference to an `annotations.rs` line in its directory tree.                                                                   | P5's steps are supplied by this document (see below)                                                                                                                  |
| G2  | **All three ownership assignments for the `include!` refactor have zero implementation.** RFC-039 lists it under P4; `02` assigns the reference to itself; `06`'s S7 also lists it. However, `02`'s change list (6 additions + 20 modifications) **does not include `semantic_tokens.rs`**.                                                                             | The implementation steps are assigned to §P5 5.1 in this document (after P4 is complete); references in RFC-039 and `06` have been updated to point here              |
| G3  | **Three concurrent CI-script inventories coexist.** RFC-039 lists four `scripts/ci/*.py`; `01` lists four scripts + one `cargo test`; `07` lists four differently named checks; `04` also requires `check-synth-boundary.py`. After deduplication, there are **at least nine new scripts and no unified inventory**; `check-obligations.py` is owned by both P4 and P9. | The unified inventory appears in §P9 of this document (11 scripts, each owned by one phase); all other documents now refer to that table                              |
| G4  | **Conflict over the survival of `precedence_inline.rs`.** `06` counts it among the “1005 lines that must be revived,” while B6 in the same document says it “must be deleted together with the `Precedence` enum”; `05` advocates deletion.                                                                                                                             | **Revive in P1, delete with Pratt in P8** (decision registered as C1)—first restore CI coverage, then delete the file in P8 under 8.6 together with the dead ladder   |
| G5  | **Conflict in the wording of the L2 objective.** RFC-039 and this directory's index say “grammar-driven syntax,” but `05` self-assesses: **“It does not use true grammar-driven parsing (LALRPOP); it only implements declarative table lookup.”**                                                                                                                      | Converged on **complete LALRPOP grammar-driven parsing** (`05` §Syntax: Complete Grammar-Driven Parsing); G7/G8 acceptance gates are executed against that commitment |

## Target Design: Phase Sequence

```
P0  仓库维护机制与代码放置规程     08        ← 新增，先立规矩
P1  复活孤儿测试并修缺陷           06 §S1
P2  建立等价性判据基线             07 全部
P3  修正确性漏洞（最小方案）        02 §S3-漏洞半部
P4  阶段契约与统一 Driver          02
P5  checker 文件内拆分             02 + 本文补齐
P6  类型表示单一化                 03
P7  中间表示 SSA 化                04      } 可并行
P8  前端范式变更                   05      }
P9  防反弹门禁（含统一脚本清单）     01 §防反弹 + 08 §可机检规则
P10 其余清理与状态修正             06 §S2/S3/S4/S5/S6
```

**Rationale for adding P0**: the three prohibitions (do not invent constructs; do not keep filling
indefinitely; do not patch when a refactor is required) constrain every action in P1–P10. The rules
must be established before work begins; otherwise, P1 would already add `mod` declarations to
`tests/mod.rs` while reviving tests—which is precisely what caused the G4 conflict.

all invocation paths for the exclusive tier are mutually exclusive; a budget shortfall must be reported and must not be silently skipped; **Owning phase: P0; Blocks critical path: No**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

Fix = reduce `stderr` to `diagnostics: [[code, file, line], …]` + retain only **crash marker lines** (panic / ICE / backtrace headers), adding `strip_ansi` (the run side carries color codes) and position lookahead (positions remain detectable when source snippets appear between a diagnostic header and `-->`); `exit` / `stdout` / `check.*` / `expectation` remain unchanged                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | 0.5.1                                   | The 356-line baseline was regenerated and `check-corpus-parity` showed an empty diff; **rerunning the probe after changing locales still produces zero diff** (justified and empirically verified); mutation check: changing one `run.exit` to 99 reports one diff; cost = the corpus layer no longer verifies runtime prose, which is instead covered by locales unit tests / `yx explain` snapshots / generated-output reconciliation; **Owning phase: P0; Blocks critical path: No**                                   |

follow-up slice of #486)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | 0.5.1                                   | The pre-commit `cargo test` hook no longer runs the entire suite directly (currently `cargo test --all` runs everything; AGENTS.md's “Common Commands” section specifies the fast/full channels and the red line against running the full `--ignored` suite); CI and the pre-commit local path use the same channel criteria; the 2600 tier's peak and duration are measured rather than extrapolated; `test-specification.md` contains the corresponding details section; **Owning phase: P0; Blocks critical path: No** |

> **There is no size-baseline task.** The 2026-10-03 decision removed all line-count / size
> guardrails; scale problems are to be solved through separation of responsibilities (see `08`
> prohibition 2 criterion A, manual judgment). **0.2.1 is this phase's core acceptance criterion**:
> if the guardrail cannot report the known parallel representations, the criterion design is wrong
> and the guardrail is false.

### P3 Correctness Defects (Minimal Solution)

Source: [02](02-stage-contract.md) §Implementation Points S3, defect-fix half + **the 2026-10-07
silent-drop-point audit (3.4)**

> **Semantic-correction routing (D59, user decision 2026-10-11)**: if this track or a later phase
> discovers a defect for which “the old behavior did not count as success”—**A silent failure / B
> silent mismatch / C undefined behavior and resource loss** (crash, stack exhaustion, silently
> incorrect answer)—it **may be fixed alongside the refactor**, but all four conditions are
> mandatory: ① red before green (the red criterion exists first and is genuinely red); ② do not
> alter the externally observable behavior of programs that previously compiled successfully and ran
> correctly (they must not become failures, and existing diagnostic codes/messages/exit codes must
> not be removed or changed); ③ register in the same commit (the row identifies the error class +
> the phase's revision note + the PR's mandatory “Error Type” field); ④ regenerate the baseline in
> the same commit + manually review the diff. **D user-visible semantics / E external-contract table
> / F RFC-level design changes must still receive user adjudication first.** The expanded criteria
> and A–F whitelist appear in [07 §Semantic-correction routing criteria](07-equivalence-oracle.md);
> **“default super-refactor followed by after-the-fact registration” is not accepted** (the
> rationale is in RFC-039 D59: with no red criterion, repair and regression cannot be distinguished,
> and this conflicts with “no C5′” and “no relaxation of any kind”).

      and the false rejection came from a **precheck** of unknown names in the
      metavariable-resolution block (reading the first candidate's name table from the metavariable
      array and ignoring successfully bound slots); after typecheck was fixed, ir_gen exposed a
      second layer (`lookup_param_info` searched by leaf name in `fn_param_names`, with the last
      same-name overload overwriting the earlier one, so a decision hit on `Temp.pick` bound
      according to the final candidate's parameter name, `Temp.pick#1`). Fixed surface: the precheck
      moved before slot binding and listed the union of all candidates' parameter names in the
      available column; the fallback path filters metavariable-compatible candidates by “name
      compatibility” (sharing `names_support_named_args` with the decision loop, single criterion),
      and the signature of the unique compatible candidate emerges with it (E1002 is attributed to
      the candidate owning the name); ir_gen indexes by mixed names and looks up mixed names first.
      Red before green: the frozen parent commit `10a2dbcb` produced 4 red tests → `4941f443`
      produced 23 green tests (acceptance matrices 9/9b/10/11, `named_args.rs`); corpus differential
      had zero diff (the D59 class-C premise holds); fast guardrails passed in two waves, clippy had
      0 errors, all four CI-specific guardrails passed, and error-code generated files were
      regenerated in the same commit after line shifts; review passed all 8 items (measured on the
      frozen revision by the main-line representative—the review sub-agent died twice from
      infrastructure quota/concurrency limits, and that orchestration deviation was recorded

**Acceptance**: vulnerability criteria turn green, and **removing the fix must make them red
again**. **Risk**: adding E4018 to the multi-file path is a breaking change.

**3.4 acceptance**: 3.4.1 / 3.4.2 must have red criteria before code changes; 3.4.3 is a manual
review; 3.4.4–3.4.8 use the C2/C3 and P9 9.2 guardrails as criteria. **Risk**: the new diagnostic in
3.4.1 is a behavior change and requires synchronized corpus-baseline and CHANGELOG updates.

> **Stop-the-bleeding path (2026-10-05 decision)**: P3 need not wait for all of P2. The only hard
> prerequisites for 3.1/3.2 are **2.3.2 (multi-file corpus layer) + 2.4.1/2.4.3 (two red
> criteria)**; 2.1/2.2/2.3.1/2.3.3 (IR verifier, snapshots, single-file differential, performance
> baseline) can proceed in parallel with P3. Stopping the correctness defect (`Sorted(3)` silently
> passing) must not be blocked by snapshot-infrastructure construction. 3.3 (panic to diagnostic)
> has no prerequisite and can land at any time.

### External Track RFC-029g: Remove `pub` and Automatic Binding (External Track / #399 — Ordering Constraint D56)

Source: [RFC-029g](../../rfc/accepted/029g-remove-pub-and-auto-bind.md) (accepted 2026-10-02,
implementation not started), [#399](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/399). **This track
is not a design item of the refactor itself; it is a specification-conformance deletion forced by an
ordering constraint**; it is distinct from P10 S5 (“reduce the visibility of `pub` items,” i.e.
narrowing Rust `pub(crate)`).

**Why it must be completed before P4 as a whole**: 029g's deletion surface overlaps the rewrite
surfaces of four refactoring phases. Reversing the order means repeating the same deletions and
baseline updates on the new grammar / checker layout / SSA IR.

| ------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

| AST `is_pub` ×2 (`Assign` / `TypeDefinition`)                       | `parser/ast.rs:247/270`                                                                                            | **P6 6.5 AST dead variants** + P8 + D1 directory rename (`parser/ast.rs` → top-level `src/ast/`)                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `auto_bind_to_type` and the `collect_exports` pub branch            | `typecheck/checker.rs:2150/3802/3836`, `environment.rs:284`                                                        | **P5** (`include!`→true `mod` and checker splitting are **pure moves**; pub branches will be moved unchanged into the new files)                                                                                                                                      |
| Dead-code exemptions `exempt_pub` / `is_exported` and role dispatch | `typecheck/passes/dead_code.rs`, `module/orchestrator.rs:389-407` (inside `check_project`)                         | **P4 4.2.2** (`check_project` slimming)                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `semantic_tokens`'s `Public` modifier                               | `typecheck/checker/semantic_tokens.rs:450/498`                                                                     | **P4 4.2.6** + P5 + 4.6 (D55)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `ir_gen`'s `is_pub`-ignoring binding                                | `middle/core/ir_gen.rs:1378`                                                                                       | **P7** (SSA conversion of `ir_gen.rs`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| 31 `pub`s in `src/std/`                                             | `std/list.yx` (25) / `json.yx` (4) / `option.yx` (1) / `result.yx` (1)                                             | No phase changes them directly, but **P6's hard acceptance requires zero differences in `src/std/` (`git diff --stat src/std/` is empty; direct-drive test files under `tests/` are excepted under P6's hard acceptance section)**—they must therefore land before P6 |

**Additional benefit**: 029g deletes the dead `env.exports` table (populated by the producer and
read nowhere in the repository) and the `is_exported`/`is_visible` accessors ⇒ one fewer “produced
but never consumed” field in P4's 4.3 obligation ledger.

      `warning-codes.md`, `guide/language-overview.md`, `guide/modules.md`; completed 2026-10-08)

**Acceptance (behavior changes; red criteria first)**:

1. **Red before green**: each of the two behavior changes must first land a red criterion before
   code changes—① `pub` changes from “silently accepted” to a parse error; ② unreferenced top-level
   bindings in Lib/Script roles newly report W1001 (pub items were previously always exempt).
2. **The test count must not decrease**: when deleting `exempt_pub` cases from
   `typecheck/passes/tests/dead_code.rs` and `is_pub` fixtures, equivalent new cases must replace
   them.
3. **Baseline in the same commit**: diagnostic-set changes (new parse error + new W1001) ⇒ corpus
   differential baseline (`tests/baselines/corpus-parity.jsonl`) and CHANGELOG updated in the same
   commit; IR snapshots are expected to have zero diff, and any non-zero diff must be explained.
4. **Independent revert unit**: the entire batch occupies one commit group and must not be mixed
   with another phase (DoD 6).
5. `cargo test` / `clippy -D warnings` / `python scripts/rfc/check_tracking.py` all green.

**Ordering**: overlaps with 3.4 files (`checker.rs`, `environment.rs`) ⇒ the two must be serialized
and cannot run in parallel; **the whole track precedes P4** (RFC-039 D56). The 029g track does not
depend on 3.4; the implementer chooses which to do first based on available context.

> **Commit cross-reference (2026-10-08)**: this section's incorporation (P3.5 chapter + main
> chain/constraint row) and RFC-039's D56 were swept into `97071c81b` together during a
> parallel-flow workspace cleanup; that commit's message records “3.4 addendum registration +
> D57/D58 decisions recorded” and does not mention this track. History is not rewritten. Following
> the existing convention (the `d5b18390` precedent, see the P2 closure note), this is recorded in
> place as after-the-fact bisect evidence.

|                             | 5.2.2 Converge the refinement entry point on “`&self` + return diagnostics,” **without leaving the impl**: `collect_refined_binding_checks` (now `checker/refinement.rs`, `pub(super)`) no longer implicitly writes errors through `&mut self`; the diagnostic set is returned upward and written at the sole caller `check_module_impl` (`checker.rs`, symbol located by search). **Reason for retaining the impl**: the only field used by this family is `self.env` (technically it could leave the impl), but moving both the entry point and the walker to free functions would move all call sites: `resolve_type_annotation` has 8 production sites (6 annotation paths + `refined_walk_stmt` + `collect_refined_var_names_in_stmt`), and `collect_param_refinements` has 2 production sites + 1 test site; this would create a reverse dependency from the “retained surface (signature collection)” to refinement, contrary to this step's goal of eliminating coupling | 5.2.1         | C1 zero-diff; four **symbol criteria**: ① the signature contains no `&mut self` ② the function body contains no `env.errors` ③ **the returned** diagnostics are written only at the caller ④ the entry point still has one location |
| 5.3 Extract `annotations`   | 5.3.1 Move out the 10-item annotation-validation family (now the single file `checker/annotations.rs`; `check_annotation_type_names` is the `pub(super)` entry point, the other 9 items are private to the family, and `is_predicate_head` is a free function within the family)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | 5.2.2         | C1 zero-diff                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
|                             | 5.3.2 **`collect_used_in_type` (`pub(crate)`) remains `pub(crate)` and under the `checker` module path**: it is a **shared-surface** function (consumed by `collect_function_signature` and `inference/statements.rs:16`), and 5.3.1 **did not migrate it** as measured ⇒ the “zero caller changes” criterion **was already satisfied**, so **do not add** a new `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` re-export. **Constraint**: if it is later migrated with the family (to annotations or another batch), `checker.rs` must add `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` so that the existing import remains byte-for-byte unchanged                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | 5.3.1         | **Zero caller changes** is this step's hard acceptance criterion: `git diff src/frontend/core/typecheck/inference/statements.rs` is empty                                                                                           |

**Hard constraint**: `collect_used_in_type` is used across modules by `inference/statements.rs:16`.
**If** it is later migrated with the annotation family, **`checker.rs` must add
`pub(crate) use annotations::*;`** so that the import remains unchanged; 5.3 did not migrate it, so
this batch did not add a re-export (the criterion was already satisfied).

**Split only confirmed large blocks.** Do not split modules of only 200–400 lines—they add
navigation cost, reduce cohesion, and provide no decoupling benefit. The remaining submodules
(signatures / type_defs / imports) await the files growing back to 1,500 lines.

incorporated into T2** (decision 1C)—the comparison target does not exist (RFC-011 has no built-in type-name table), so the checker and **all 16 old registrations** (3 `T3.D2C` + 13 `T3.C2D`) were voided, with no placeholder left in code and no T3 section in the report or `--json`; ② **T1 changed from warning to `panic!`** (decision 2A)—deliberately introducing a zero-construction variant must go red, while **retaining the empty-table whitelist** (`REGISTERED_ZERO_FORWARD` and `DEAD_VARIANTS_03` are now empty tables; the registration mechanism itself remains and can be injected, allowing new zero-construction variants to be registered after conversion to hard failure); ③ **T1.6's systematic +1 was attributed to a single point** (decision 3A's survey conclusion)—the source was the `Type::without_fn_param_names` added by the 6.5 batch (AST→AST faithful reconstruction, previously miscounted as a parser forward variant), and the criteria were changed to table-driven form under 03 §5.7 “single source of reconstruction locations,” with the baseline numbers and criteria snapshot refreshed in the same batch. **All remaining surface was handled in the continuation** (see 09a §P6 6.6 continuation): ④ **the parser's non-Fn signature fallback**—`src/frontend/core/parser/statements/types.rs:823`'s `extract_fn_type_info` still uses let-else to return `Type::Name{"()"}` (changed from the `Type::Void` fallback in 6.3; formatter output remains byte-identical, but this path is **syntactically reachable**: `semantic_tokens`'s `Name` arm records one extra dummy-span token compared with the old `Void` arm)—it must be eliminated (the parser must no longer produce a pseudo-type as fallback); ⑤ **the three `ast::Type` carriers in `ir.rs`**—`Instruction::Cast { target_type }` (`src/middle/core/ir.rs:325`), `Instruction::TypeTest { ty }` (same file `:330`), and `FunctionBody::TypeDecl { definition }` (same file `:651`) **still hold `ast::Type`**; 6.3 only deleted variants, 6.4 only introduced injection, 6.5 only reshaped representations, and 6.6 only changed guardrails, so **none of the four batches changed the IR shape**, and carrier disposal requires another round (it would move IR snapshots and the corpus baseline)—**handled on 2026-11-01**: `Instruction::Cast` / `TypeTest` were deleted along with two zero-producer dead variants under WBS 6.6.11 (the bytecode-side `BytecodeInstr::Cast` has the same name but a different meaning and is retained); `FunctionBody::TypeDecl.definition` was replaced under WBS 6.6.12 with the IR-native narrowed carrier `TypeDeclBody` (field table + bytecode type-table entry) ⇒ **the IR layer has zero `ast::Type` carriers**; ⑥ **leg-B round-trip coverage must be committed**—the reverse bridge B (`mono_to_ast_type`) output's full round trip through `From` back to `MonoType` is currently covered only by a probe test in the verification tree (not committed), so a separate implementation-side small task must add it permanently; ⑦ **two built-in closed sets remain in the parser**—`src/frontend/core/parser/ast.rs:1084`'s `CONST_PARAM_TYPES` and `src/frontend/core/parser/probe.rs:121`'s `PREDICATE_NAMES` (the B6 inventory has two locations under `scripts/ci/check-boundary.py`); the injection seam is in place (`src/driver/helpers.rs:1317`'s `parse_with_probe` production use), and it must be evaluated whether the default `parse()` entry point (9 production entry points according to `src/frontend/core/parser/probe.rs`) can all be injected with `SemanticProbe` so that `BuiltinProbe`'s built-in closed set can be deleted; ⑧ the ranges of `substitute_type_ast` / `substitute_type_in_ast` are AST-derived, and neither documentation nor prose may hard-code line numbers—(**both were deleted under 6.6.11 / 6.6.12, so this item is void**); ⑨ **snapshot coverage ≠ corpus-file count** (**completed**, 6.6.16): **the regression's point of origin = t4 (`28ded1fd8`)**—that commit moved the parser's three built-in name tables out of L2, and **all 9 production entry points were injected with `SemanticProbe`, except the snapshot criterion**: `middle/core/tests/snapshot.rs::normalize_corpus_file` still used bare `parse()` (`NullProbe`) ⇒ `Terminates` was no longer recognized as a predicate ⇒ a return-position measure parameter was excluded as a const generic parameter ⇒ `ir_gen` reported E3006 (“variable b could not be resolved”) ⇒ canonicalized text returned `None` ⇒ the criterion **silently continued**. **Measured criteria**: before t4, **216/216 full coverage, 0 skipped**; after t4, **214/218** (4 skipped: `deep_recursion_within_limit` / `explicit_termination_measure` / `measure_decreasing` / `stack_overflow_e6004`). “`corpus-parity.jsonl:133` records `diagnostics: [] / exit: 0`” and the snapshot path's E3006 do not mean “the two criteria disagree about the same file”—the corpus baseline is produced by the CLI/driver, which had already injected the probe, so the snapshot criterion itself supplied the wrong AST. **Both original errors were corrected**: ① it was neither “pre-existing” nor “present since 2026-10-12”; ② the proposed option “fix ir_gen's E3006 path” was **withdrawn**—ir_gen's error is correct; the criterion supplied the wrong AST. **Fix** (criterion only, zero product-code changes): the entry point becomes `parse_with_probe(&tokens, &SemanticProbe)` (same criteria as the driver); replace the relaxed `assert!(compared > 200)` lower bound with a **reconciliation assertion**—every file for which `reaches_ir_generation` is true but no canonicalized text is available must **fail and be named individually**, and `compared == reachable` must be asserted; the update process must also make skipping red (otherwise stale snapshots would be neither compared nor updated). After the fix, **218/218**, and `measure_decreasing.snap` converged to the narrowed-carrier shape (`type_decl []`—the type body contains only a const-bound expression and no data fields), leaving zero span residue in the snapshot surface. Measured: restoring bare `parse()` makes the new assertion red and lists the four files above (coverage 214/218) |

**6.9 Model and Invariants (First-Principles Rewrite, 2026-11-13)**

> **Model**: D62's end state is a **unified item world**—type positions and value positions share
> one set of compile-time items. One strength declaration produces one quantifier reading
> `q ∈ {0,1,ω}`; `q` is the **sole input** to slot allocation, and the call-site argument view and
> implementation-header binding view are two projections of the same fact. Equality is a **pure
> decision** (identical after normalizing both sides; does not bind type variables); evidence is the
> **sole entry point** for q=0 slots (four sources: literals/value environment/single-value
> pinning/Γ equality); erasure is the **runtime consequence** of q=0 (it holds for closed terms and
> open terms/recursion); Σ construction sites **reuse** the same evidence interface rather than
> defining a separate one. **Completion criterion = the invariants hold, not that substage names
> have been completed.**

> **6.9 closure criterion**: I1–I5 and I7 all hold; I6 closes under the 6.9.5 design-review
> conclusion (does not block P7); I8 has evidence for every slice; residual items
> #537/#539/#540/#541/#542/#552 all have a disposition (fixed/explicitly excluded/separate issue).
> **Do not substitute “all substages checked” for the invariants holding.**

> **Partial order (not a serial chain)**: A facts → {A.C3, B.d, C.2, D design}; B kernel → {B.d,
> C.2, D implementation}; C.1 is independent and complete; F4 decision → A.C3; decision-point
> protocol → B.d; design review → D implementation. A.C3 and B.d can run in parallel but require
> file-surface coordination (C3 leans typecheck→mono, d leans types/solver→sema); C.2 can run in
> parallel with B.d after 6.9.2-B/6.9.3a (it does not depend on defeq); D's implementation waits for
> confirmation of the B design package; it depends on A (q=0 erasure) and C.1 (dispatch), and
> possibly on 6.9.3's typed exit. **P7 gate = A/B/C exits; D does not block.**

> **A.C3's prerequisite: the F4 decision (decided as A on 2026-11-16; measurements in 09a §P6
> 6.9.2-C3 F4)**: the old wording “`CheckOnly` evidence does not enter requests” is
> obsolete—`ConstEvidence`/`CheckOnly` have been deleted; the current mechanism is that open-term
> `normalize` returns `Unproven` ⇒ no request is created, and the representation layer's
> `GenericArg::Const(ConstExpr)` can hold `k-1`. **The decision now consists of two orthogonal
> decisions and one termination subdecision**: D-a representation (A: open terms enter `deferred`
> and are normalized before enqueueing / B: do not specialize); D-b evaluation point (mono only;
> C-1: the second field and C-2: reconstruction from IR both break the single
> representation/evaluator, so C-3 = A); **F4-T termination** (naive firing pushes the recursion
> chain to `max_depth` and hits E3005; T-a: fold conditions within the instance / T-b: bounded
> firing + fallback to the original at the boundary / T-c: termination layer or spec change). **Run
> the M1–M6 measurements in 09a §P6 6.9.2-C3 F4 first, then decide; do not treat “A is
> implementable” as a conclusion before measurement. The user's decision A (2026-11-16):
> D-a=A-closed, D-b=C-3, F4-T=T-b, bound=8; C3 implementation is a separate task, and the complete
> form of I4 is not claimed.**

> **B.d's prerequisite: the decision-point protocol (not yet written)**: RFC-027 §12.1 states “the
> decision point first follows unification; after unification fails, definitional equality may be
> used, but all bindings produced by the unification attempt must first be rolled back.” The
> implementation must answer: whether the entry point is an outer wrapper around `solver.unify` or a
> new `decide` layer; whether defeq is attempted only when both sides are ground (without unbound
> variables); the snapshot/restoration boundary and cost; and whether unify and defeq share one
> budget or have separate budgets. **Responsibility boundary**: unify continues to handle
> subtyping/Any/Never; defeq only decides equivalence and does not take over subtyping;
> bidirectional subtyping must not be treated as equivalence (RFC-027 §12.1). **Provide the protocol
> and production call graph before writing code**; 6.9.3c's wiring contract wasted two rounds
> because it assumed `can_assign` was the decision point, while `AssignmentChecker` has zero
> production instantiations.

> **E acceptance and residual work (I8, cross-cutting)**: every slice must provide ① zero
> corpus/IR-snapshot drift or item-by-item classification; ② `testsuite run fast` + affected
> integration tests; ③ error-code generator idempotence (if emission points/code tables changed); ④
> behavior changes classified under D59 and red before green. **Wiring tasks additionally require**:
> production call-graph evidence (which production entry point, how many paths, which path is
> reachable); unit-test reachability must not be substituted for user reachability; rollback and
> budget require measured numbers. **Residual disposition**: #537 (class D, proof-function parameter
> refinement silently dropped; not fixed before user adjudication, and 6.9.4/6.9.5 must not depend
> on its repair), #539 (import-surface q=0; class-C stage boundary, so 6.9.4 covers only in-module
> behavior until closed), #540 (deep-nesting stack overflow; **closed**—3.20.30 closed on
> 2026-11-14: orchestration-layer 64 MB large-stack thread + parser depth gate
> MAX_PARSE_DEPTH=1024 + E0019, see row 3.20.30), #541 (TailCall; **closed**—on 2026-11-16 three
> sites entered and exited together: `call_form_is_rewritten` / `replace_calls_in_function` /
> `drop_erased_call_args` through the single point `rewritten_call_target`, covering
> `Call`/`TailCall`, with init sections and function bodies sharing one site-rewrite source;
> red→green + three mutations; see 09a §P6 6.9.2 #541), #542 (isolated shared layer; **separately
> scheduled**—the user's 2026-11-16 decision B places it after P7 batch a and before post-mono
> production guardrail wiring for DCE; the original/shared layer is not currently deleted, no D38
> exemption is written, and the guardrail timing is unchanged; 6.9 **remains open** pending
> completion; evidence and review points are in 09a §P6 6.9.2 #542), #552 (cross-module execution of
> imported multi-parameter predicates; adjacent to 6.9.5, separate issue). **Ownership pending
> adjudication**: `rfc027_refined_transparency`'s four open-constraint tripwires (`Eq(N, n)`) do not
> belong to 6.9.4's constant-equality bridge; leave them under 6.9.4 outOfScope or move them to
> 6.9.1/E4018's proof-function surface, subject to adjudication.

> **Non-claims/boundaries**: this item covers only record Σ (B: witness field), does not touch
> Π/`DepFn`, and does not claim the complete Π/Σ form; 6.9.4 covers only constant equalities and
> does not fulfill the open-constraint `Eq(N,n)` tripwire; `f(y)(y)`'s second value position is
> outside this item; witness erasure ≠ Runtime check erasure; if #537 is not fixed, 6.9.4/6.9.5
> cover only shapes that do not depend on proof-function parameter refinement; if #539 is not fixed,
> 6.9.4 covers only in-module behavior; #540 is closed (3.20.30); #542 must land or be exempted
> before `verify` is elevated to a guardrail.

**Hard acceptance** (**criteria revised 2026-10-27, inheriting the user's 2026-10-26 decision ②**):
`git diff --stat tests/yaoxiang tests/baselines src/std/` must be **empty in every round of
6.1–6.7**. **Direct-drive test files under `tests/`** (tests that drive changed code, such as
`tests/formatter_integration.rs`) **may be modified when mechanically required**, but each change
must be registered with **file / line count / reason**; **tests must not be deleted and assertions
must not be weakened**. **Exception**: 6.8.2 is a **behavior-fix** batch (new diagnostic code +
baseline update), does not fall under this hard acceptance criterion, and runs as a separate round
with separate adjudication.

> **Revision relationship**: the original criteria (the user's 2026-10-26 decision ②) read
> `git diff --stat tests/ src/std/`, making the entire `tests/` tree zero-diff; the revised criteria
> **change only direct-drive test files from “zero diff” to “modifiable with registered file / line
> count / reason”**, while the **zero-diff requirements for the corpus layer (`tests/yaoxiang`),
> baseline layer (`tests/baselines`), and standard library (`src/std/`) remain unchanged**. First
> application = 6.1.2: only `tests/formatter_integration.rs` changed by **3 lines** (2
> `assert_format_eq` occurrences changed from `i64`→`Int64` + 1 comment line), because the changed
> spelling requires synchronized expected type names in those assertions; no tests were deleted and
> no assertions weakened. See 09a “P6 6.1 Name Canonicalization Closure.”

### P7 SSA-ize the Intermediate Representation

Source: [04](04-ssa.md) §Implementation Points batches a–d, criterion C4, **strictly serial within
each batch**.

- [ ] **7.0 Pure-move split (P7 prerequisite batch)** (6 third-level tasks; registered 2026-11-12,
      user approved insertion before 7a)

> **7.0 design basis and boundaries**: the target layout comes from the directory tree and migration
> table in [01-routing](01-routing.md)—`middle/ir/ir_gen.rs` → `middle/lower/`,
> `sema/inference/expressions.rs` → `sema/infer/` (call-decision family `call/`). This batch
> performs **pure moves only**, with the acceptance criterion “zero semantic diff except paths”
> (manual file-by-file review) + C1 snapshot zero-diff. It does not conflict with
> [RFC-039](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md) alternative A (“split files only, do
> not change the paradigm”), which was rejected because it used moving as a **substitute** for
> paradigm work: this batch is paradigm work's **prerequisite**—7c.1's `lower/synth.rs` depends on
> the `lower/` directory being in place, and 7d.1's `generate_call_expr_ir` method-level split is
> performed on the smaller files after the move. On the expressions side, the user chose option A
> (split only the call-decision family; `statements.rs` remains in place); the wholesale
> `inference/` → `infer/` rename and merging statements are outside this batch and require another
> design round after P7. `synth.rs` is a new boundary rather than a move and belongs to 7c.1. Survey
> baseline (2026-11-12): `ir_gen.rs` is 9,804 lines with 148 methods, primarily one
> `impl AstToIrGenerator` block (about 9,250 lines); `expressions.rs` is 6,825 lines with 93
> methods, primarily one `impl ExpressionInferrer` block (about 6,280 lines); the largest methods
> are `infer_call_expr` at 1,671 lines and `generate_call_expr_ir` at 866 lines (the latter is the
> 7d.1 target).

- [ ] **7b Switch to the SSA form** (6 third-level tasks; 7b.1 was split into 1a/1b under DoD 6)

non-equivalence, that must be resolved before the 8.5 switch, not hidden afterward with C5′. Missing
one of the approximately 89 BP-reference migrations in 8.6 will **silently change associativity**
(without an error, only the parse result changes).

> action code references `sema` in reverse, `check-boundary.py` will block it.

to CI in their “introduction phase”; report-only scripts are converted to hard failures at P9 (from
“report” to “fail”)—existing violations (parallel representations, disambiguation aliases, etc.)
must wait until P6/P8 to reach zero; converting them early would leave CI permanently red and cause
guardrails to be bypassed manually. At P9 conversion, the guardrails should be exactly green. P2's
2.5, P4's 4.3.2, and P7's 7c.1 refer to this table and do not define separate inventories.

## Implementation Points: Dependencies and Parallelization

### Mandatory Serial Main Chain

`P0 → P1 → P2 → P3 → P3.5 → P4 → P5 → P6 → (P7 ∥ P8) → P9 → P10`

> **P3 stop-the-bleeding path (2026-10-05 decision)**: P3's prerequisites narrow to **2.3.2 +
> 2.4.1 + 2.4.3** (multi-file corpus layer + two red criteria). The rest of P2 (2.1 IR verifier, 2.2
> snapshots, 2.3.1/2.3.3 differential and performance baseline) can proceed in parallel with P3.
> Correctness-defect containment is not blocked by criterion-infrastructure construction; however,
> **P4 must still wait for all of P2**—the unified Driver requires all three layers of criteria to
> be in place.

| Constraint                | Reason                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| P0 → P1                   | The three prohibitions constrain every action in P1 (1.2.x adds `mod` declarations to `mod.rs`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| P1 → P2                   | Reviving tests changes the test count and corpus baseline                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| P2 (after narrowing) → P3 | The vulnerability criteria must **be written red first** (2.4.1/2.4.3), and the multi-file corpus layer (2.3.2) must exist so the red tests have somewhere to run                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| P3 → P4                   | Fix bugs before refactoring; the reverse order causes bugs to be fixed into the stage table as “established behavior”                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| P3.5 → P4                 | RFC-029g (#399)'s deletion surface overlaps the rewrite surfaces of P4 4.2.2 / P5 / P6 / P7 / P8 (the list appears in §P3.5 of this document)—the reverse order means repeating the same deletions on the new grammar, checker layout, and IR (D56)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| P4 → P5                   | The `include!` refactor and checker split modify the same file                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| P5 → P6                   | Consecutive changes to the same file must be separated, otherwise regressions cannot be bisected                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| P6 → P7 / P8              | If type representations do not converge first, the new IR grows into a third representation; **P7 gate = exits of 6.9 stages A/B/C** (I1–I3, I5, I7; namely 6.9.2 including C3, 6.9.3 including d, and 6.9.4 complete), with D (Σ) not blocking; P7 may consume the quantifier-derived views and defeq interface but must not create a parallel ledger; the 7.0 pure-move batch is a P7 prerequisite and is not bound by this gate, but must not consume undecided defeq/quantifier interfaces |
| P7/P8 → P9                | 9.5's snapshot baseline and 9.3's `pub(crate)` leak count can take their final form only after P7/P8                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |

### Parallelizable Groups

| Group                                        | Members                                                                            | Basis                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| -------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A (the only group authorized by RFC-039)** | P7 ∥ P8                                                                            | Disjoint file sets: `ir.rs`/`ir_gen.rs`/`bytecode.rs`/`translator.rs` vs `lexer/*`/`parser/*`                                                                                                                                                 |
| B                                            | 1.2.1 ∥ 1.2.2 ∥ 1.2.3 ∥ 1.2.4                                                      | Each independently adds a `mod`; revert file by file                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| C                                            | 2.1 ∥ 2.2 ∥ 2.3.1 ∥ 2.3.2 ∥ 2.4                                                    | The three layers of criteria are independent                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| D                                            | 4.4 (proof-layer closure) ∥ the entire P6 line                                     | `02` explicitly states under S5 that this “should proceed independently of S1-S4”                                                                                                                                                             |
| E                                            | 4.2.5 ∥ 4.2.6 ∥ 4.2.8                                                              | None depends on another after 4.1                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| F                                            | 9.3 ∥ 9.9 ∥ 9.10 (the three scripts introduced in P0 can be developed in parallel) | 9.5's snapshot baseline must be taken last                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| G                                            | S3 ∥ S4 ∥ S5                                                                       | Disjoint file sets (verified 2026-10-05: S3 changes `docs/src/dev/design/check/` + `config.js`; S4 changes `docs/src/rfc/` + the `check_tracking.py` generator; S5 changes two `pub`s in `src/frontend/core/typecheck/`; no pairwise overlap) |

**Non-parallelizable (file overlap)**: P1(1.1.x) ∥ P8(8.0.x); 1.2.1 ∥ 8.3.2; 6.0 ∥ S2 (B6/E5 use the
same files); 4.3.2 ∥ 9.2 (now unified under P4); **3.4 ∥ P3.5** (`checker.rs` / `environment.rs` are
the same files—the external track's pub-deletion batch and the silent-channel fixes must be
serialized).

**New internal documentation constraints**: 6.0→6.1→6.2→6.3→6.4→6.5→6.6 is strictly serial; 7.0 →
7a→7b→7c→7d is strictly serial (7.0 is P7's prerequisite pure-move batch), and 7b's `Phi` arm must
precede 7d.

## Key Decisions and Rationale

- **P0 is an independent phase rather than part of P9**—guardrails are “after-the-fact checks,”
  while rules are “before-the-fact judgment.” P9's scripts cannot prevent “patching when a refactor
  was required”; only the D0–D4 review checklist can.
- **P9 is the sole authoritative inventory of CI scripts**—the original G3's three concurrent
  inventories made closure impossible. This table merges them into 11 scripts, each owned by one
  phase, with report-only status and conversion timing explicit.
- **P3.5 incorporates RFC-029g rather than leaving it as an “independent track to execute after the
  refactor”**—its deletion surface overlaps the rewrite surfaces of P4/P5/P6/P7/P8 (RFC-039 D56).
  Deferring it means repeating the same deletions on the new grammar, checker layout, and IR after
  the refactor; this is the same lesson as incorporating `2315`/`2341` into 7e and correcting RFC
  status under S4.
- **P5's steps are supplemented by this document rather than folded into P4**—P4 is already the
  largest single-point risk, and adding checker splitting would exceed the granularity at which work
  can be rolled back.
- **5.3.2 uses “zero caller changes” as its hard acceptance criterion**—the split must be
  independently revertable.

## Known Limitations and Risks

- **1.3.x (fixing exposed defects) has no regression criterion**—it only says “the fixed defect must
  not be rolled back,” and lacks a requirement to record what was fixed. **Execution convention**:
  every 1.3.x fix's commit message must reference the fixed test name and defect description,
  providing after-the-fact bisect evidence.
- ~~**7b.1 granularity is too coarse**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: split into 7b.1a / 7b.1b as
  exclusive commits under DoD 6.
- **8.2.1 granularity is too coarse**—about 89 renamed references; missing one **silently changes
  associativity**.
- ~~**D17 / D20 have no corresponding third-level tasks**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: D17 landed as
  7e.1; D20 landed as 4.5.1/4.5.2; D32/D34 opcode reclamation was incorporated into 7e.1, with the
  deletion action left under P10 S6.
- ~~**The `2315` / `2341` data-loss defects were originally assigned to “separate issues”**~~
  **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: incorporated as 7e.2 / 7e.3. The exception table (throw/try) is core
  language semantics and must not be left as an unimplemented residual.
- ~~**E2 (`lib.rs:568/582` `todo!()`) has no phase ownership**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**:
  incorporated under P10 S2.
- ~~**F7 (opcode guardrail extractor) has ambiguous ownership**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**:
  incorporated into P10 S6's generation-time guardrail task.

## Conflict Registration: All Adjudicated

> **The original “Conflict Registration” C1–C8 and the open questions in the other documents have
> all converged into the
> [RFC-039 Decision Registry](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md) (D1–D50).** No
> unresolved items remain in this document. Itemized correspondence:

| Original ID | Issue                                                                               | Decision                                                                                                                                   |
| ----------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| C1          | `precedence_inline.rs` is revived in P1 and deleted in P8                           | **Revive** (P1's premise is that revival produces real assertions); P8's 8.6 deletes the file together with Pratt                          |
| C2          | “Grammar-driven” commitment vs table-driven implementation                          | **Completed in full**: LALRPOP is used; see `05` “Syntax: Complete Grammar-Driven Parsing”                                                 |
| C3          | Conflict between C6's category definition and S1's classification                   | **S1 belongs to C6** (revival requires only regression tests, not equivalence criteria); C6 is expanded to “pure deletion or pure revival” |
| C4          | Whitelist for `test_release_plan_spans_consumed`                                    | **No whitelist**; the difference must be empty; if it cannot be, the `ReleasePlan` contract is defective ⇒ change to `PlanId` (D20/D41)    |
| C5          | Internal differences between the two `Aggregation` functions have not been verified | **P4's 4.2.7 lists this as a mandatory verification item**, to be checked line by line during implementation                               |
| C6          | The wasm playground fix requires P4's `ProgramKind`                                 | **The wasm-path vulnerability fix is incorporated into 4.2.8**; P3 handles only the three native entry points                              |
| C7          | Ownership of `method_def_ordinals`                                                  | **Assigned to 04** (P7 batch c), see D21                                                                                                   |
| C8          | Correction of `upvalue_count` and the `.42` version number                          | **Fix + change `VERSION` from 4 to 5** (the format header already has `MAGIC` + `VERSION` fields, `codegen/bytecode.rs:14-16`), see D17    |
